## Appendix E. The second repair round (revision 4)

Six findings from two reviewers (render, wiring) and one (UX). Each was reproduced on the earlier code before anything was changed, the root cause was fixed, and a test was written that fails on the earlier code (`tests/audio-repair2.test.mjs` for the logic; `scripts/audio-check.mjs` for the render; the real page in headless Chrome for the controls).

| Finding | Verdict | What was done |
|---|---|---|
| render A1: the oscillator budget counts voices that are not sounding yet, and counts before the sparkle goes | Verified (a pioneer sale with xl coins, 108, plus a district opening, 63, made 171; the resolver's "also" tick, 2 s later, was dropped with `dropped = {}`; 6 of the 84 cause and reward combinations lost it; the real peak of those bursts is 45 to 61) | the budget is the most that is alive at one instant (`layerSpans`, `peakAlive`): each layer is a span from the voice's real start, only the instants when the new voice is alive count, only the layers a voice builds count (the family skip, the secondary's texture and a dull voice's ding are left out), a bank's partials end one by one, and a tiny voice that still does not fit is dropped as `osc`. 3.3 states the budget as it is built, and its claim about when it is reached is corrected |
| render P1: a sale's pan is on the cause alone | Verified (a sale at 0.6: whole burst R-L +0.4 dB, the law 9.8; the coin carries 9 dB more energy than the sale; at 1.0, -0.5 dB) | a member of a `playMix` with no pan of its own takes the first member's; `pan: 0` keeps a member centred; a reward sentence is a mix of its own and stays centred. `audio-check` renders a sale at 0.3, 0.6 and -0.6 through the real engine and holds R-L to 2 dB of the law (it reads 4.3, 9.8, -9.8) |
| render S1: the loss family's fall lives under 250 Hz | Verified (18 variants flat or rising through the laptop model, none with a fall above 250 Hz; the body was one held D4) | `body(t, g, note, d, from)` steps down, A4 to D4 or F#4 to D4; `clock` pause is a marimba pair A4 F#4; `alert.insolvent`'s slide starts at A4 and its body steps are 40 ms off the strike (the peak fell 2.8 dB); trims recalibrated (17 variants moved by 0.1 to 2.8 dB). The contour test now holds the 18 to a fall of 1.5 semitones in 250 to 800 Hz and through the laptop model |
| wiring W1: a silenced reward is rung by its toast | Verified (the bounty toast played `ui.ok` after the resolver dropped `daily.done` at 4x, 16x and 64x) | `toast(msg, level, ms, { own: true })` writes `data-snd="own"`; the quest, achievement, daily bounty and Codex toasts carry it; the observer skips it |
| ux U1: a preset resets the volume to 70 | Verified | `applyPreset` leaves the volume (only 0 goes to 70); the engine's copy and the Classroom rule of a room follow; the tests that expected 70 were changed |
| ux U2: the speaker button and M at volume 0 | Verified ("Sound is on. Press to mute", "Sound on.", an ok nobody can hear) | `toggleMute` and `speakerState` (`settings.js`): pressed means something can be heard; at volume 0 a press brings it to 70 and says "Sound on at 70 percent."; with both buses off nothing changes and the words say why. Used by the speaker button, key M and the Deal Room's switch |
| ux U3: the Sound section keeps the old state when M or the speaker changes it | Verified (Mute box unchecked under "Sound is off.") | the engine's events bring every control to the settings (`refresh`), not the status line alone |
| ux U4: a card the engine drops is silent and says nothing | Verified (Classroom: `quiet`; Interface off: `bus`) | `whyDropped` names the rule; the audition page writes it on the status line and the card (`silent: ...`), and has Reset to Full |

Nothing here was a matter for ears except the body's step (S1): the fall is measured, not heard. A listening pass on a laptop speaker (10.5) should confirm that a fifth of 60 ms is read as a "no" and not as a doorbell.


## Appendix F. The third repair round (revision 5)

Eight findings were left open by the third verification. They were taken by two tracks and integrated on `v2/audio-r2`: **F.1** is the engine and calibration track (I1 the start click, I2 the quiet-laptop hierarchy, I3 the footsteps, W1 the run end at 64x, A2 the volume), **F.2** is the experience and discoverability track (A1 the Classroom rule before the Draft, A3 the audition page, A4 the repair dial tone). They touch different files: F.1 `palette.js`, `recipes.js`, `engine.js`, `settings.js`'s taper and the calibration; F.2 `wire.js`, `audition.js`, `dialtone.js`, the Deal Room and the dashboard. The integration check (no double playing from the new toast and the Classroom helper) is at the end of F.2.

### F.1 Engine and calibration

Five findings of the third verification (render, wiring and UX reviewers). Each was reproduced on the earlier code (903dfa5) before anything was changed, the root cause was fixed, and a check was written that fails on the earlier code: `tests/audio-clicks.test.mjs`, `tests/audio-calibration.test.mjs` and `tests/audio-r2e.test.mjs` for the logic, and `scripts/audio-check.mjs` for the render (`--calibrate --pools` writes the trims, the plain run only checks). The other findings of that round (A1, A3, A4: the Classroom rule before the Draft, the audition page's bursts, the repair dial tone's toast) belong to other tracks and are not touched here.

| Finding | Verdict | What was done |
|---|---|---|
| I1: a noise layer leaks the first sample of the shared noise at a start on a sample boundary | Verified, and worse than reported. With every layer muted (a gain of 1e-9, so that anything heard is a leak), started at 16 times the engine really starts a voice at (`q * 128 / sr + 0.012`, plus a shift of 0 to 15 ms in whole milliseconds) at 44.1 and 48 kHz, **409 of 414** variants, pool members and one-shots leaked more than -90 dBFS; the worst was -9.5 dBFS at 48 kHz (`cash.in` xl) and -23 at 44.1. The cause is the one found: a gain node's own value is 1, the envelope's first event was `setValueAtTime(0.0001, T)`, and a frame at T that the event misses by a rounding hair passed the source at full scale. The same hazard was in every other first event: a biquad's frequency (350 Hz until its event), an oscillator's (440 Hz), the beds' crossfade, the modifiers' fade and the repair dial tone's fade | `env()` puts the value in place first (`param.value = 0`, then the event), `biquad()` and the oscillators set their frequency first, and a new `fadeIn()` does the same for the bed, its modifiers and the dial tone. The release of a layer quieter than the -80 dB floor no longer rises to the floor (it did, which made a muted layer an audible one). **Result: the same sweep leaves nothing over -148.7 dBFS at either rate.** `tests/audio-clicks.test.mjs` holds the shape in Node on a fake context that logs `.value =` (every first event has its value put in place first, for every variant, pool member, one-shot, bed, modifier and the dial; the check can see the fault: 4 of 7 fail on the old start); `audio-check` renders the sweep (`--leak` alone). **Not tested: Safari and Firefox** |
| I3: footsteps are calibrated on one noise offset and play 6 dB apart | Verified (`world.step` a and b, 32 plays with only the noise place and the pitch varying: Fast level spread 5.3 and 7.2 dB, 7.3 and 9.9 dB with the engine's gain jitter, 95th percentile of the peak -6.0 and -6.8 dBFS, the loudest -3.6 against a ceiling of -6). Two causes: the buffer is plain random samples, and a footstep is five milliseconds of noise | **The noise is built in the frequency domain** (3.2): every 10 ms block has the same magnitude spectrum with random phases, blocks overlap under a sine window and wrap, and it is made once at 48 kHz for every device (24 ms, once, at the first gesture in Chrome; it was made by the first sound). That alone took the footsteps from 5.3 and 7.2 to 4.4 and 5.5: five milliseconds of noise through a 700 Hz band has about nine independent values in it and no buffer can make that even. So **the calibration now measures every noisy sound at 32 places in the noise** (the median is the level, the highest the peak), and the sounds that move more than 1.5 dB with the place (`world.step` a and b, `amb.door-bell`, `shutter`, `flap`, `cricket`, `thunder`) get a list of the 8 places that put them nearest the median, which the engine picks from by the voice's seed. **Result: the footsteps move 0.8 and 1.4 dB with only the noise and the pitch varying, 1.6 and 2.1 dB with the gain jitter (0.7 dB either way is the jitter's own 1.4), the 95th percentile of the peak is -7.9 and -7.8 dBFS, the median play is 0.1 dB from the calibration.** The seed bias of the others is gone with the medians (amb.steam +2.7, amb.door-bell +2.1, cam fit +2.1 in the review) |
| I2: the loss, refusal and alert family sits 4 to 8 dB under its tier for a quiet laptop | Verified (measured with the A-weighting added to `dev/measure.js`, which reproduces the review's figures to 0.1 dB: tiny -39.3 to -29.9, medium -33.2 to -22.6, loud -23.7 to -14.8; the tiers overlapped by 3.3 and 1.2 dB, 13 mediums under the loudest tiny) | The second model is a calibration and a test next to the K-weighted one (3.2, `scripts/audio-calib.mjs`): each variant is centred between the two, and the check fails a sound outside 3 dB of its tier's median under any, tiers that overlap under any, a loss under the loudest sound of the tier below, and a loss louder than its gain under either. The family's body moved up (2.2: D5 to A4, A4 to F#4, a held A4; a wood knock below 350 Hz carries its octave); the low taps and thumps that only a flat speaker plays were made quieter where they dominated; the six alerts (`alert.advance` 3, `alert.shock` fed and tariff, `alert.margin-call`, `alert.insolvent`, `run.end` horizon) took the body with a small knock and, for the two tolls, a sustained fifth, because they are held at their -3 dBFS ceiling by their crest factor and their level could rise only by lowering their peak; `deal.result` bad, `audit.result` fail, `bid.hammer` lost and `repair.done` rough, which stage 2 had put above their gains on the K-weighted Fast, are 1.5 dB under them (`VARIANT_TRIM`), `cash.out` s 1 dB and `sale.scam` 2 (it was 3). `bid.rival` out is a glide D5 to A4. The map now has `crew.client` ent, `haggle.anchor`, `haggle.tell`, `read.probe` power, `pitch.angle` utility, `ui.ask` danger, `ui.crate`, `ui.dialog-close`, `haggle.move` hold and split, `buy.lot`, `clock` pause and resume and `alert.advance` 2 at registers a quiet laptop plays. **Result, below** |
| A2: the default is quiet and the bottom third of the slider is dead | Verified (at the default `ui.tick` light peaked at -21.4 dBFS and `level.up` at -10.1 through the chain; 5% was -52 dB) | The taper is `(v / 100) ** 1.5` and a headroom of +4.64 dB sits between the slider and the glue compressor so that the default of 70 is unity (3.2); Classroom has none. The status line says "very quiet" below 25% (`VERY_QUIET`). The numbers are in 3.2: the tiers reach -31.7, -29.3, -24.1 and -18.3 at the default (they were about -39, -36, -30 and -23), 100% is 4.6 dB above, the 12 loudest sounds together still peak at -3.0 dBFS, and Classroom is 10.6, 14.5 and 13.9 dB under Full at the same volume. `tests/audio-r2e.test.mjs` holds the taper, the headroom, the engine's gain, the clip and the words, and that the document states them |
| W1: `run.end` done is lost to the exit's start at 64x | Verified (the settlement comes 48 x 7 / 64 = 5.25 s after the start, inside its 8 s; a test that fails on the earlier code drives the sequence through `eventCalls` and `noteCalls` into the real engine) | `run.end` is in `CD_PER_VARIANT`: the start, the settlement and the horizon have a cooldown of 8 s each. The same cause twice inside 8 s is still one fanfare; the done fanfare preempts the start's moment (3.4.7) as before. Tested at 1x, 4x, 16x and 64x |

**Tiers before and after, under both models** (Fast level of the non-pool variants, dB; minimum, median and maximum; 102 tiny, 64 medium, 22 loud; "clear gap" is the quietest of a tier less the loudest of the one below). Before is 903dfa5 with its own trims, measured by this round's `dev/measure.js`.

| Model | Tier | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| K-weighted Fast | tiny | -32.3 / -30.0 / -28.1 | -31.4 / -29.9 / -28.0 |
| | medium | -24.8 / -23.0 / -21.1 | -24.0 / -23.2 / -21.2 |
| | loud | -16.1 / -14.6 / -12.9 | -15.6 / -15.0 / -12.9 |
| | clear gaps | 3.3, 5.0 | 4.1, 5.5 |
| K-weighted Fast after the 220 Hz high-pass | tiny | -33.0 / -30.5 / -28.2 | -31.6 / -30.0 / -28.8 |
| | medium | -26.2 / -23.8 / -21.5 | -24.4 / -23.6 / -22.4 |
| | loud | -17.8 / -15.7 / -13.3 | -16.3 / -15.5 / -14.3 |
| | clear gaps | 2.0, 3.7 | 4.4, 6.1 |
| **Quiet laptop** (220 Hz high-pass, then A-weighting, Fast) | tiny | -39.3 / -32.8 / -29.9 | -34.9 / -32.3 / -31.4 |
| | medium | -33.2 / -25.9 / -22.6 | -28.5 / -25.7 / -24.7 |
| | loud | -23.7 / -17.6 / -14.8 | -19.9 / -17.4 / -16.7 |
| | clear gaps | **-3.3, -1.2** | **3.0, 4.8** |

The widest variant of a tier under the quiet laptop was 7.3 dB below its median (`buy.lot` broken, medium; 6.5 for `pitch.angle` utility, 6.1 for `alert.shock` tariff) and is 2.8 (`sale.scam`, which is 2 dB under its gain by design); 13 mediums were at or under the loudest tiny sound and none is; the standard deviation of a tier under it went from 2.7, 2.9 and 2.7 to 0.8, 1.1 and 0.9. The largest `bal` (the move stage 2 made) is 3 dB; 3 variants (the pool of `haggle.move` large) are at it.

**What is not done, and what only ears can say.**
- **Ears.** The body of the family moved from 294 and 440 Hz to 440 and 587 Hz. It is measured to fall, to be in key and to be heard on a quiet laptop; whether a falling fifth at 587 to 440 Hz still reads as "no" and not as a doorbell is not known. The same for the held fifth under `alert.insolvent` and `run.end` horizon (the tolls now ring over a sustained A4 and D5), the thumps that are 40 to 70% of what they were, the footsteps that start from one of eight places, and whether 4.6 dB of drive into the soft clip at 100% sounds like a louder game or a dirtier fanfare. 10.5's listening plan decides.
- **Other engines.** Safari and Firefox were not run (I1 and the noise). The fix for I1 is the one the Web Audio specification asks for (a value in place before the first event); the noise is made in JavaScript and its buffer is the same in every engine, but the resampling of a 48 kHz buffer on a 44.1 kHz context is the engine's own.
- **The sweep is offline.** `audio-check` starts its voices where the engine does (a whole number of 128-frame quanta plus 12 ms) in an `OfflineAudioContext`; a real `AudioContext`'s `currentTime` was not read in a page. The beds' and the dial tone's fade-in is covered by Node tests of the code that calls `fadeIn` and by a Chrome render of `fadeIn` itself, not by a render of a bed's crossfade.
- **Two models are not all listeners.** The quiet laptop is a high-pass and an A-weighting; there is no equal-loudness contour of ISO 226, no measured speaker and no 400 ms or 3 s integration window (EBU momentary and short-term), which the review also quoted. A listener at a high level or on good speakers hears the K-weighted figures.
- **Only ten sounds are played 32 times** (`PLAY_PROBES`); the others rely on the median of their 32 places in the noise. Only seven sounds have a list of places; a new noisy sound that moves more than 2.5 dB with the place fails the check and needs one (`--calibrate` writes it).
- **The volume.** A saved volume now plays louder (a 70 is 6.2 dB louder than it was, a 30 is 10 dB); a build that never shipped has no saved volumes to be surprised, but the slider's numbers mean something new. The ambience has the taper without the headroom, so the bed sits 1.6 dB louder against the effects at its default. At 100% the gaps between the tiers are 4.7 and 5.1 dB (the check asks for 4.5 there and 5 at the default). The "Play a test sound" button exists in the Sound section; the first-run hint that offers it (A2's other suggestion) is not built, and neither is the question of 11.3 whether the first run starts muted.
- **Pools** are calibrated one by one as before; the pool of `haggle.move` large is at the clamp of the balance (3 dB), the others are inside it.

*(Fourth round: the residual figures of a panned loud sound and of the volume at 100%, which this section leaves to the ear, are recorded in Appendix G, R8.)*

### F.2 Track X: experience and discoverability

Three of the third round's open findings belong to what a first-time player and an instructor meet: A1 (a class that joins by code), A3 (the audition page's silent buttons) and A4 (the repair dial tone nobody is told about). The other five (I1 the noise-layer click, I2 the 40-phon hierarchy, I3 the footsteps' seed bias, W1 the run end's cooldown, A2 the default level) belong to the engine and wiring tracks and are not touched here: this track edits no line of `palette.js`, `recipes.js`, `engine.js` or the taper and defaults of `settings.js`. Each finding below was reproduced on the earlier code first; each has a test that fails on it (`tests/audio-r2x.test.mjs`, `tests/audio-r2x-page.test.mjs`) and a walk in headless Chrome with the real page and a real `AudioContext` (`node scripts/audio-r2x-check.mjs`, 63 checks).

| Finding | Verdict | What was done |
|---|---|---|
| **A1** a class that joins by code hears the Draft's fanfare at full volume | Verified (`main.js` joins the room inside `onBegin`, after the Draft; the rule ran only when the game mounted; `draft.begin` is loud, 1.6 s). Beyond the report: the press on **Join** itself made the "Sound effects are on" note, one press before the room's note, so a student was told two things about sound | `pendingRoom(room)` (7.1): the Classroom rule applies from the Draft on, restored by Back, told once; the Join press makes no first-minute note; the dashboard has a **Quiet sound link** box (`&sound=quiet`, off by default, read at boot and never over a student's own choice); its settings button says **Display and sound** (it said "Access") and the dialog is titled by the same name (it said "Accessibility and sound"); FACULTY_KIT.md and COMPLIANCE.md have a Sound section. Measured: `draft.begin` with a pending room is 10.00 dB under solo play; in Chrome the rule is on while the Draft is open, the note is one, Back puts the settings back, and a link flag never overrides a saved choice |
| **A3** the audition page's bursts go silent in sequence with no word; beds too | Verified, and the cause is wider than the report. The page read drop reasons **synchronously** and only four of them (`mute`, `bus`, `quiet`, `locked`), but the collector counts its drops one macrotask after the press, so `budget` could never have been seen. And `budget` is one word for four rules: the reward budget, the moment queue (a sale with an xl coin pressed inside the last fanfare was dropped `budget` while the budget was open), the tick gap, and the speed thinning. A budget reset alone would still have left a burst silent: a level-up rung 1 s earlier is dropped by its own 2 s cooldown. On the earlier page, run by the new test on a virtual clock, **173 presses were silent with no word** across its 394 buttons in nine configurations of the bar (every bed with Ambient off, every one-shot under a mute, the dial tone in Classroom, Stop, the minute) | `js/audio/audition.js` (now `dev/audition.js`): `explainDrop` names every reason the engine can count, and tells `budget` apart by the state (the seconds to the next full reward, "a big moment is still playing, N s more"); `pressBurst` reads the outcome after the collector and its delayed part have run (both flushes), names the sounds the collector dropped, and says when rewards "played as a small tick". The **Fresh budget before each burst** box (ticked by default) puts the reward budget back, waits out the last big moment and asks the burst's sounds to skip the per-sound cooldown and draw from the player's bucket, as a press does; unticked, the real rules apply and are explained. Presses queue, so two quick presses are two bursts in order. Beds say "Ambient is off: tick Ambient in the bar", mark the button silent and look again after 450 ms before claiming anything on a first press; one-shots, the dial tone (Classroom, mute, Game off, already running), Stop, Reset, the minute (a summary: how many sounds, what each rule held back) and a run of sounds ("2 of 12 did not sound") all say. The status line has a row of its own (a long reason wrapped the bar) |
| **A4** the repair dial tone is off and nothing tells anyone | Verified (no user-visible string; the toast 4.5 promised was never built; the track is `aria-hidden`). Two findings under it: a **toast could not have worked** (painted over by the modal sheet, 4.5), and **`announce()` was not heard while any dialog was open** (the page's live region is not in the accessibility tree, Chrome: `activeModalDialog`), which silenced the Sound toggle's "Sound off.", every preset's announcement, the test sound's and the Deal Room's own | `js/audio/dialtone.js` and the Deal Room: a **Dial tone** switch beside Sound, a line under the dial, a description on Stop, a labelled group round the picture, one announcement per run; `announce()` also writes inside the open dialog. The default stays **off**, with the reasons in 4.5. Walked in Chrome: the region inside the sheet is a `status` node in the tree and `#live` is ignored; Stop's accessible description is the line; a click on the switch starts the real tone at once and a second lets it go; in Classroom it says why; under reduced motion nobody is pushed and the switch is there |

**What was measured.** (1) `draft.begin`'s voice gain with and without a pending room: -10.00 dB (the game bus under Classroom, 3.2). (2) The earlier audition page: 173 silent presses with no word (394 buttons, nine configurations of the bar, virtual clock, fake context, the page's own code, with only a `say` hook added so the test can drive it). (3) The burst reproduction on a fake clock, pressed 1.2 s after each other: without a fresh budget the second burst plays nothing and the third plays nothing; with it every burst plays, the longest wait for a big moment is 613 ms. (4) In Chrome, with the real engine: six bursts pressed in the author's order each play (5, 4, 1, 2, 2 and 4 voices); with the box unticked the third says "Reward budget: ... the next full one is 3 s away". (5) The accessibility tree of an open Deal Room: `#live` ignored (`activeModalDialog`), the sheet's own region a `status`.

**Tests.** `tests/audio-r2x.test.mjs` (18): the pending-join rule and the gain, the Join press, the link and the dashboard, the dialog's title, the docs, every line the room can say about the tone in every state, the switch's one rule, once per run, the markup of the Fix stage and the header, the real session's announcement, switch and Classroom, `announce()` inside a dialog. `tests/audio-r2x-page.test.mjs` (8): the page's own code on a page stand-in, a fake context and a virtual clock; the bursts in order with and without the box; **every button in nine configurations of the bar sounds or says why** (a first visit, muted, Interface off, Game off, World ticks off, Classroom, volume 0, Ambient on, Ambient on at level 0); the beds, the dial tone, Stop, Reset, a run of sounds, the minute. Three source-level tests of the earlier rounds followed the code (the deal DOM half has four direct sound calls, the two switches' own tones; the page names `explainDrop`).

**Not done, and what only a person or another track can do.**
- **Toasts raised while any modal dialog is open are painted under it** (the Deal Room's "Not done", a verification result, the Settings' own). `#toasts` is outside the top layer. The fix is the shell's: make the container a manual popover (`popover="manual"` and `showPopover()` while a dialog is open) or move it inside the dialog. It touches `css/styles.css` and `css/shell.css`, which this track did not edit; the sound layer no longer depends on it.
- **No screen reader was run.** The claims are from Chrome's accessibility tree (roles, names, descriptions, the ignored reasons), not from NVDA, JAWS or VoiceOver; whether a region inserted 30 ms before its text is read the same way by all three is a listening test (10.5). Safari and Firefox were not walked.
- **The default of the tone** is decided (off) and defended in 4.5; it is still the author's to overrule by ear.
- **A reload in the middle of the Draft** leaves Classroom on until the next Draft, Resume or game mount (the rule is saved with the settings, like a room's, and `applyRoomRule` restores them the next time it runs with no room). A student who reloads and joins again is in the state they expect; one who reloads and then plays solo hears the quieter game until then. A `pagehide` that lets a pending join go would also fire for a page the browser keeps and brings back, so it was left out.
- **The link flag persists** in the student's browser (it is a sound choice, `touched`); a *room-scoped* link flag that is put back when the student leaves would need the `sound` field on the room protocol that 11.3, item 2, names.
- I1, I2, I3, W1 and A2 of the open list are the engine's and the wiring's.

### F.3 The two tracks together (integration)

The tracks were written apart (F.1 touches the voice, the calibration and the volume; F.2 the wiring, the Draft and the Deal Room) and were merged on `v2/audio-r2`. What neither could show alone:

- **Nothing plays twice.** `scripts/audio-r2x-check.mjs` now counts what the real engine plays, by id, across the sequences the new code touches (63 checks): the **Join** press and the Draft that opens with the room's note play `{"ui.tick": 1}` (the note is an `info` toast, and an info toast rings nothing; the only toasts that ring are good, warn and a refusal, and a toast carrying `data-snd` is left alone by the observer); **Begin** plays `{"draft.begin": 1}` with no click tick and no toast `ok` over it; one press of the Deal Room's **Dial tone** switch plays `{"ui.toggle": 1}` (the button is `data-snd`, so the page-wide click table leaves it alone, and its own tone is the same single request the Sound switch makes). A game that mounts after the Draft finds the rule already applied (`applyRoomRule` acts only on the way in and the way out), so its own check makes no second note, and its first-minute hint is not made in Classroom. `announce()` writes to a live region and plays nothing. Counted on the merged tree in headless Chrome: solo Draft to game plays `{"draft.begin": 1}` and the one hint toast; a room plays the same with the room's note instead.
- **The Draft's fanfare in a pending room, through the whole chain.** F.2 measures the voice: `draft.begin` is 10.00 dB under solo on the game bus. F.1's volume has headroom that Classroom does not (3.2), so through the master chain at the default 70 the loud tier is **13.9 dB** under Full at the same volume (interface 10.6, medium 14.5), and the Draft's fanfare is the first thing a class hears. Both are true: 10 dB is the rule, the rest is the headroom the quiet mode does not use. (Since the fourth round the rule is 8 dB for the game: 8.00 on the bus, 12.0 for the loud tier and 12.5 for the medium through the chain, interface unchanged; Appendix G, R3.)
- **One merge conflict**, in this document only: 11.3 took its first item from F.1 (the default of 70 is unity gain since revision 5) and its second and third from F.2 (the Classroom rule from the Draft, the dial tone's decision); Appendix F is one appendix with F.1 and F.2 under it.

## Appendix G. The fourth repair round (revision 6)

The verification of the third round (`v2/audio-r2`, 30748e4) found 34 things in four reviews: render (R1 to R8), wiring (W-1 to W-9), the real page in Chrome (B1, B2) and a first-time player and an instructor (U1 to U15). Six of them are the same fault seen twice (the repair tone that never comes back: W-2, B1, U1; the two notes in a student's first minute: W-5, U3; the notes that name a button the Draft did not have: W-8, U7). Each was reproduced on the earlier code before anything was changed, or its report was checked against the code and the engine's own output; the root cause was fixed and a check was written that fails on the earlier code (`tests/audio-r4.test.mjs`, the additions to `tests/audio-r2x.test.mjs` and `tests/audio-r2x-page.test.mjs`; `scripts/audio-check.mjs --calibrate --pools` writes the trims, the plain run only checks and, run on the written trims, passes; `scripts/audio-r2x-check.mjs`, the browser half, makes 97 checks where it made 63). **Not taken as reported: W-7** (the engine never writes a settlement as "$1.25M"), **the remedy proposed for R8** (it makes the panned sound quieter, measured), and three taken in part (W-6, U6, U12).

### G.1 What was found and what was done

| Finding | Verdict | What was done |
|---|---|---|
| **W-1** an Academy lesson undoes the room rule | Verified in the code: `wireGame` ran `roomCheck` for the lesson's sandbox, whose session has no `roomInfo`, and took that for leaving the room | `roomCheck` returns for a game with `sandbox`: a rehearsal neither joins a room nor leaves one. The real game's mount finds the rule in place and makes no second note. The browser leg (`scripts/audio-r2x-check.mjs`) plays lesson 1 in a room and asserts Classroom stays on |
| **W-2, B1, U1** the repair tone is gone for the rest of the stage after a mute, a hidden tab or Game sounds off, and the room says it is on | Verified: `engine.sync()` and `setHidden()` end the tone, the room's `ses.toneOn` stayed true, `dial.update` does nothing without a tone, nothing started it again | `retone()` checks the flag against `audio.dial.active` and starts the tone as soon as it can be heard, from every engine event (the existing listener) and every frame of the dial. 4.5 |
| **W-3** the first three door bells of a run: one plays | Verified: the resolver let them through and the id's 30 s cooldown dropped the second and the third | `gateClock` sets `noCooldown` on the first three (`resolve.js`); the fourth waits for the gap as before |
| **W-4** a press that also closes the dialog plays two voices (Open debrief, Academy replay, Join) | Verified: the click's tick, then the dialog observer's close 50 ms behind it; `unwire`'s 40 ms fade also cut the tick | The observer adds no close when a control with a sound of its own was pressed in the last 250 ms (Escape and the backdrop still get theirs); `release(keepMs)` leaves a voice of the last 150 ms to finish |
| **W-5, U3** a student who types the code is told two things about sound | Verified: only a press on Join itself was exempt | A press in the Join panel, a `?room=` address, a code in the field and a key that makes no sound (Tab) no longer make the hint, and the hint is not spent. 7.4 |
| **W-6** distance is not wired | Verified: `where` was set nowhere; the buyer's pan is the district's block | Wired for the events the player did not cause and whose district is known (a manager's cycle, freight arriving, customs): 8 dB down behind 2.5 kHz when the district is not the player's. **Not wired:** "off screen" and the buyer's own place (the `pitch` event has no buyer): written in 3.6 and in `js/audio/README.md` |
| **W-7** the settlement amount is read as $1.25 for $1.25M | **Not as reported.** The settlement note is written by `engine.js` (`money()`: "$1,250,000 settled into cash."), not by `fmtMoney`, and it parses whole at every size (checked on the real engine at $900, $1.25M, $48M and $2.3B). The report's repro fed `fmtMoney` text, which this note never carries | Nothing was broken. `amountIn` reads a K, M or B suffix anyway, as a guard, and a test holds the engine's own text at four sizes |
| **W-8, U7, U15** the notes name a Display and sound button the Draft does not have, and do not mention M; M announces in a browser with no Web Audio | Verified | The Draft's header has a *Display and sound* button; the room's note says "Press M to mute, or change it under Display and sound."; M does nothing without Web Audio |
| **W-9** FACULTY_KIT says the link "ends ?sound=quiet"; row 88 says `inst.cue` is off in a room | Verified (the link gains `&sound=quiet`; nothing gates the ceremony) | Both reworded to what the code does; whether `inst.cue` should be off stays 11.3 item 4 |
| **B2** a script-made `pointerdown` creates a context Chrome refuses | Verified | `unlock` ignores events with `isTrusted === false` |
| **U2** AUDIO.md sends the author to the prototype's page | Verified | Three references now say `/dev/sounds.html`; the prototype's page carries a banner; 10.5 says which tool of the page does which item |
| **U4** *Stop everything* leaves the bed playing | Verified | It unticks Ambient and stops the bed, and says so |
| **U5** a card pressed while a fanfare rings is silent | Verified (18 of 263 in order) | A card press passes `preempt`: it cuts the moment still ringing (the game never asks for it) |
| **U6** the dashboard's quiet link | (a) Verified: the button read the box at draw time. (b) Verified: the flag marked the student's settings as their own, for good. (c) The control stays: the room rule does not cover the title screen | The box is before the button and the toast says what the link carries; a link with a room code is the room's rule applied early and goes back when the student leaves (7.1) |
| **U8** the first-run toast covers Begin | Verified (rectangles overlap at 1366x768 and 1280x720) | Toasts sit above the Draft's footer (`css/ui-draft.css`) |
| **U9** Stop's description is forty words | Verified | Stop is described by a dozen words; the long line is under the dial in the first repair of a run only |
| **U10** the room's switches fail Label in Name | Verified | The name is the visible word, the state a badge and `aria-pressed`, and the pressed switch looks different |
| **U11** the dial tone is the quietest thing, the first loud sound is the Begin fanfare | Needs ears | Not changed: see G.3 |
| **U12** the audition page lacks the tools the checklist asks for | Verified | Contents list; the modifiers say whether they are on; **Mono** and **Laptop speaker** boxes; a **Blind round**; the variant's own clause of the recipe on its card. Variant-specific meaning lines are not written for every variant (the recipe clause is shown where the recipe names the variant) |
| **U13** the test button is at the bottom | Verified | The test button and the status line come right under the preset and the volume |
| **U14** the instructor's guidance is thin | Verified | FACULTY_KIT: a line in the five-minute setup and a before-class checklist in Sound; README: a line. A room-level force-Classroom switch is still not designed (11.3 item 2) |

### G.2 The levels and the engine (R1 to R8)

| Finding | Verdict | What was done, and the numbers |
|---|---|---|
| **R1** real plays of the loud tier pass the ceiling | Verified: with 32 plays the way the engine makes them, 13 variants were over (`zone.open` t4 +1.8, `perk.bailout` +1.1, `run.end` horizon +1.1, `alert.shock` fed +0.7) | The ceilings hold the 95th percentile of those plays (`peakJ`, `m.sweep`), margin 0.5 dB; the check draws its own seeds. After: p95 at most 0.3 dB under the ceiling, the largest peak of all 0.1 dB under; the loud tier's level is 0.5 dB lower (mean -18.0), the gap to the medium 5.6 dB at the default and 5.0 at 100% (5.0 and 4.5 are asked) |
| **R2** the tiers nearly touch under longer integration | Verified: the quiet laptop's 400 ms momentary loudness, tiny to medium 1.1 dB, medium to loud 2.7 | Third model (`lapMomA`); `body()` rings longer (2.0 d instead of 1.4); `buy.lot`, `haggle.close` accept and `sale.scam` and `alert.shock` tariff were redrawn with a longer, lower-knock body; `sale.herd` h moved its energy from under 250 Hz into its body. After: 2.4 and 4.6 dB; every variant is within 3 dB of its tier on all three models, less its own softness |
| **R3** the tiers narrow at the speaker and in Classroom | Verified: the interface bus's two mediums met the loudest tiny game sound (1.1 dB at the speaker); Classroom, 1.8 dB under the laptop model | `acad.teach` right and `draft.pick` complete take back 1.5 of the bus's 3 dB; Classroom is 8 dB for the game (10 before; `CLASSROOM_DB`): a room is 12.5 dB under Full for a medium game sound and 12.0 for a loud one at the default volume. `audio-check` holds the clear gaps at the speaker (10.1). The Draft's fanfare in a pending room is 8.00 dB under solo on the bus |
| **R4** a change of district dips 4 to 7 dB | Verified (and by arithmetic: 3 s in and 2 s out on linear gains of uncorrelated noise is -5.1 dB at 1.39 s) | Equal-power curves (a sine in, a cosine out) of one length (`AMB.CROSS`, 3 s; `equalFade`), in 16 linear pieces, with the first value in place before the first event (I1). After: 0.9, 1.0, -0.1 and 1.2 dB over four pairs |
| **R5** two losses louder than their gains | Verified | `sale.reject` fake and `bank.draw` b are in the list (14 pairs) with a softness of -1.5; the calibration holds every pair after stage 2 (`holdTwins`, 0.3 dB under on both Fast levels) |
| **R6** the herd and the scam have no falling note a laptop plays | Verified | `sale.herd` h falls A4 to F#4 and `sale.scam` D5 to A4 in the body; both are in the contour test (20 losses) |
| **R7** the play-to-play limits are checked for ten sounds | Verified: 10 variants moved more than 2.5 dB (`alert.insolvent` 3.7, `cash.in` xl 3.5, `run.end` horizon 3.3, `world.step` b 3.2 on the laptop model). The pitch alone moved the Fast level of the layered ones by 2 to 2.5 dB (their unisons overlap in time) | The loud tier and the xl coin take 0.4 of the pitch jitter, `alert.insolvent` 0.2 (`jitterOf`); the lists of places in the noise are chosen from 64 plays (32 before) by the spread of both Fast levels; the check plays every variant 32 times. After: 2.3 dB at the most |
| **R8** at 100% the loud sounds pass 0 dBFS before the compressor; a panned loud sound is quieter | Verified, and safe: nothing passes -3 dBFS | **The remedy proposed (-1.5 dB when panned) was measured and is worse**: a loud sound at pan 0.6 through the chain is 0.6 to 0.9 dB under the same sound centred at the default volume and 0.9 to 1.4 at 100% (both channels, K-weighted Fast); with 1.5 dB less it is 1.8 and 2.0 dB under. The compressor and the clip take more off the hot channel, and less drive means less of that, but also less sound. Clamping the pan to 0.4 would recover about 0.2 dB. Nothing was changed; the figures are held by `audio-check` (at most 1.6 dB under, peak under -2.85) and are the author's by ear (G.3) |

### G.3 What only ears can say

- **Classroom at 8 dB for the game, not 10.** The fourth round made the interface-to-game and tiny-to-medium gaps clear in a room (R3) and paid for it with 2 dB: the Draft's fanfare in a pending room is 8 dB under solo on the bus and 12.0 dB under Full through the chain. Is a room of thirty laptops still bearable at that? If not, the other lever is the interface, which would give back 2 dB of the gap.
- **The herd and the scam.** They fall now where a laptop plays (and the herd has less low content); are they still "hollow money" and "swindled"?
- **A body that rings longer.** Every loss and refusal has a body (`body()`) whose tone rings about 40% longer. A small speaker plays that tone; is it a "no" or an "mm"?
- **The repair dial tone (U11)** is the quietest voice (6 dB under a tiny sound, by design: 4.5), and it is the only non-visual way to time a repair. It is off by default. If it is too faint on laptop speakers for the player who turns it on, give it its own level; this was not changed without ears.
- **The first loud sound (U11).** A solo player's first loud sound is the Begin fanfare at the default volume. A softer first-run fanfare, or a hint before it, is a taste question (11.3 item 1).
- **A panned fanfare** (R8): 0.6 to 1.4 dB under the same sound centred, and at 100% the loud tier passes 0 dBFS before the compressor and is cleaned by the clip (as the verification measured: a residual of 31 to 44 dB under the best linear copy for a loud sound panned 0.6, and 46.6 dB under for a burst of sale xl and a margin call): a louder game, or a dirtier fanfare, is the same question as F.1's.
- **Safari and Firefox** (10.5 item 7) are still not listened to.

## Appendix H. The fifth repair round (revision 7)

The verification of the fourth round (`v2/audio-r2`, 113accd) found six things: one by the wiring reviewer (W2-1) and five by a first-time player, an instructor and a player with low vision (U1 to U5; the numbers are this round's own, not the fourth round's U1 to U15). Each was reproduced on 113accd before anything was changed, in headless Chrome with the real page (W2-1, U1 and U5 by hand first, with the numbers the reviewers gave; the browser check below, run on that commit, fails for each of the six, with the same figures: the header at y = -118 at XL and 5 px from the edge at Large, `ui.ok` for a copy that did not happen, the coins dropped as "quiet"), the root cause was fixed, and a test was written that fails on the earlier code: `tests/audio-r5.test.mjs` (24 tests; each fault put back in turn, one at a time, fails at least one of them) and the browser half, `node scripts/audio-r5-check.mjs` (headless Chrome, 64 checks). **The eight open items of the file the brief named (I1, I2, I3, W1, A1 to A4) are the third verification's**: they were taken in the third repair round (Appendix F), verified again in the fourth, and `scripts/audio-check.mjs --calibrate --pools` still holds them (the start-click sweep, the three models of a listener, the play-to-play limits, the tier gaps); none was reopened.

### H.1 What was found and what was done

| Finding | Verdict | What was done |
|---|---|---|
| **W2-1** Copy student link does nothing, and plays `ui.ok`, where `navigator.clipboard` is undefined (plain http on a LAN address, the shared-lab setup `server/static.mjs` documents with `HOST=0.0.0.0`) | Verified in Chrome: with the clipboard removed the click played `ui.ok` and raised no toast, and nothing was copied (`navigator.clipboard?.writeText(...).then(...)`: the optional chain short-circuits the whole line); with a clipboard that rejects, `ui.ok` at the click and then the info toast with the link; `copylink` is in the `OK` set of the control table | `copyText(text)` (`js/ui/instructor.js`): the async clipboard when there is one; otherwise, and when it refuses, the older command from the click (a read-only field, selected, `execCommand('copy')`, removed, the cursor back on the button). **`copylink` is out of the `OK` set**: it plays the tick at the click and its outcome speaks (the good toast's fallback ok, or nothing for the failure), which is 4.3's rule for a control that can fail. When nothing can copy, the toast is "Could not copy it by itself. The student link is ..." for nine seconds. **Not as proposed:** the suggested fix was the link in a toast only; a toast is `pointer-events: none`, so the link could be read and typed but not selected, and on exactly the setup the finding is about the instructor could not copy the link at all. The command makes the button do what it says; the toast is what is left when even that is refused |
| **U1** a student who touches the volume in a room is left in Classroom for good after leaving, and the remembered values are deleted unused | Verified through the real UI (`?room=ABCD`, Join, Begin, Display and sound, volume 50, Menu, Start over, Delete and restart, Play solo): `quiet: true, touched: true, master: 50, clockSounds: false`, `audioBeforeRoom` removed at the solo Draft, Display and sound showing "Classroom (quieter)" | `leaveRoom` is a three-way merge (7.1): each of the seven fields the Classroom preset sets goes back to how it was if it is still as the room set it, and stays if the player changed it; the volume, the ambience level, the world ticks and the M shortcut are carried as they are; `touched` is true only when the player chose the mix, so a volume does not exempt the next room. One note says what the sound is now ("Sound is back to Full: Classroom sound applies only in a room."), only when the rule was released and something can be heard. Start over gives the rule back at once (`onReset`), not at the next Draft. **Worded differently from the proposal** ("You left the room: sound is back to Full"): a Back out of the Draft, which was told "Classroom (quieter) in this room" before any room was joined, releases the rule too, and nobody left a room then. The test that said "changed meanwhile: kept" (`tests/audio-repair.test.mjs`) encoded the all-or-nothing rule and was rewritten |
| **U2** the test button says "then coins" and Classroom drops the coins | Verified in the engine and in Chrome: `?room=ABCD&sound=quiet`, the test played `ui.tick` and `alert.advance` and dropped one as "quiet"; `cash.in { size: 'm' }` was dropped, `{ variant: 'm' }` played. The cause is as reported: the gate read `opts.variant ?? firstVariant(id)` and `size` is resolved later, in `variantFor` | The gate asks `peekVariant`, which is `variantFor` without the rotation: by `variant`, else by `size`, else the first variant. The step is asked for by variant too, so it does not depend on the gate. The announcement says what a room leaves out ("...Classroom leaves out the smallest game sounds and the clock sounds."). **No sound of the game changes in a room**: the standalone cash sounds the wiring asks for by size are tiny ones (`cash.in` s, `cash.out` s and m: `cash.out` m is a tiny variant, which the first draft of this fix took for a medium one and its test caught), and the rest are members of a mix, which the gate never reads. Only the test button, and the legacy `kaching` alias before a game is wired, were affected |
| **U3** key M, and the speaker button, give no visible confirmation | Verified in Chrome: on the Draft, M set `mute` and changed nothing on the screen (no toast, no DOM change); only the unmute made a sound; the dashboard has no speaker button at all | One info toast, `toggleMute`'s new `shown` text ("Sound off. Press M to turn it back on."; "...Use the speaker button..." when M is switched off; "Sound on."), from key M and from the speaker button (7.3). `toast()` speaks it, so `announce()` is not also called. Inside an open sheet a toast is under the backdrop (4.5), so there the words are spoken in the sheet and no toast is raised. **Not done:** the optional speaker chip on the title, the Draft and the dashboard |
| **U4** the dial's one-time explanation is spent by a repair the player cannot start; a later one only says "Dial tone is off." | Verified in Chrome: with $0 the repair room opens with no `.dial` and the announcement carries the marker sentence and where the switch is; after Escape and $100 the dial is there and the line says "Dial tone is off. Careful needs no timing." and nothing about where the switch is | `tellOnce` and the new `dialsDrawn` are asked only for a repair that has a dial (`run.view()`); a repair that cannot be afforded announces what is true ("A repair here can run to $x and you have $y."). The first two dials of a run say where the switch is while the tone is off: the first in the long line, the second in the short one ("Dial tone is off: use the Dial tone switch next to Sound to follow it by ear. Careful needs no timing."); the third and later are as before (4.5) |
| **U5** at the largest text size the Deal Room's header, with Sound and Dial tone, is above the top of the window | Verified in Chrome at 1366 by 768, text size XL: `.deal-top` at y = -118, the two switches at y = -115, dialog `scrollTop` 149 (of 176 possible), the cause the autofocus on Stop (`scrollIntoView({ block: 'nearest' })`); at Large the report had the header 5 px from the edge (`scrollTop` 22) | From 761 px wide the header is `position: sticky` at the top of the sheet, on the sheet's own ground (a `::before` that also covers the padding above it), with `scroll-padding-top` for a control brought into view. The copy says "the Dial tone switch next to Sound", not "the Dial tone button at the top of this window". Measured after: at XL the header at y = 31 at the first paint and at the end of the scroll, Stop at 526 to 587 (the header ends at 76); at Large and Medium the same; on a phone (390 px) it is not sticky, being three rows tall |

Tests and checks. The Node suite is 1349 tests (1335 pass, 0 fail, 14 skipped: the relay's, which need `npm install` in `server/`); eight older tests encoded the contracts this round changed (key M announced and did not toast; Classroom was all or nothing; the dial line named a button "at the top of this window"; Copy student link was an ok) and were rewritten to the new ones, with the reason beside each. The browser check makes 64 checks (the third and fourth rounds' `scripts/audio-r2x-check.mjs` still makes its 97 on this branch). `scripts/audio-check.mjs --calibrate --pools` passes and writes `js/audio/trims.js` unchanged: nothing of a recipe, the palette or the calibration was touched.

### H.2 What this round leaves open

- **The asymmetry of `touched`.** A volume moved in solo play, before joining, still makes the student `touched` and so keeps them out of the automatic Classroom (7.1: "a player's own sound settings are never overridden"); a volume moved inside a room no longer does. A student who turns the volume down at home is therefore not put in Classroom in a lab, but a quiet one at the volume of their choice; whether the volume alone should count as "a choice" at all is a decision for 11.3, item 2 (a room-level switch), not for a repair.
- **A phone's header** scrolls with the sheet; at XL on a phone the Sound and Dial tone switches can be off the screen. It is three rows tall: a sticky one would take a third of the screen.
- **The note on the way out** is an info toast: it rings nothing. Whether a student who has just left a room wants to hear that sound is back is for ears.
